Also, Philippines EBITDA is adjusted for tax (i.e. after both gaming and income taxes). We use discretionary FCFs, after maintenance capex, but before expansionary capex. Melco Resorts' numbers are on an attributable basis, adjusted for minority portion. Past results are not an indicator of future performance.

## EBITDA building block – VIP vs Mass tables

<table><tr><td rowspan="2"></td><td colspan="2">VIP Gaming</td><td colspan="2">Mass Gaming</td><td rowspan="2">Comments</td></tr><tr><td>Junket</td><td>Direct</td><td>Premium</td><td>Grind</td></tr><tr><td>Gross gaming revenue</td><td>100</td><td>100</td><td>100</td><td>100</td><td>also known as net win</td></tr><tr><td>(Gaming tax)</td><td>(41)</td><td>(41)</td><td>(41)</td><td>(41)</td><td>Up to 40% of GGR, plus fixed table/slot levy</td></tr><tr><td>(Junket commissions)</td><td>(44)</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>1.25% of rolling chip (44% of GGR, based on theo 2.85% hold)</td></tr><tr><td>(Direct VIP rebates)</td><td>0</td><td>(33)</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>Typically 0.7-1% of rolling chip, or 25-35% of GGR</td></tr><tr><td>(Bad debt)</td><td>0</td><td>(3)</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>Direct VIP is the only segment that casinos provide direct credit</td></tr><tr><td>(Comps)</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>(20)</td><td>(10)</td><td>Rooms, F&amp;B, or retail consumption</td></tr><tr><td>(OPEX)</td><td>(7)</td><td>(7)</td><td>(14)</td><td>(14)</td><td>Dealer wage, ancillary costs, and etc.</td></tr><tr><td>Segment EBITDA</td><td>9</td><td>16</td><td>26</td><td>36</td><td></td></tr></table>

## Macau gaming: How does a typical casino make money?

## Valuations and risks

• MGM China: Our Dec-24 PT of HK\$18.5 is based on a property by property sum-of-the-parts valuation that implies a 10x FY24E EV/EBITDA, compared to its historical mid-cycle multiple range of 12-13x, or an 8.2% FY24E FCF yield. The multiple is lower than what we assume for its peers (11-13x), which is very cheap in our view, given its substantial share gains and great execution. Downside risks include: (1) a deteriorating macro and consumption landscape in China, which could weigh on gaming demand, as well as investment sentiment/valuation; (2) a potential clampdown on capital outflows, which, if it rises to an unprecedented level, would negatively affect Macau’s high-end segments (i.e., direct VIP and high-end premium mass); and (3) weaker-than-expected market share momentum.

• Wynn Macau: Our Dec-24 PT of HK\$11 is based on a property by property sum-of-the-parts valuation that implies a 11x FY24E EV/EBITDA, compared to its historical mid-cycle multiple range of 13-14x. The multiple is in the lower range of what we assume for its peers (11-13x), which we believe has more upside risks than downside. Downside risks include: (1) a deteriorating macro and consumption landscape in China could weigh on gaming demand, as well as investment sentiment/valuation; (2) a potential clampdown on capital outflows, which, if it rises to an unprecedented level, may negatively affect Macau’s high-end segments (i.e., direct VIP and high-end premium mass); and (3) competition in high-end mass, previously a key focus area of Wynn, may intensify as most of its peers emphasize opportunities in this segment.

• Sands China: Our Dec-24 PT of HK\$26 is based on a property by property sum-of-the-parts valuation that implies a 13.5x FY24E EV/EBITDA, compared to its historical mid-cycle multiple range of 15-16x. The multiple is higher than what we assume for its peers (11-13x), which is justified in our view given its best-in-class business mix. Downside risks to our rating and price target include: (1) a deteriorating macro and consumption landscape in China could weigh on gaming demand, as well as investment sentiment/valuation; (2) a potential clampdown on capital outflows, which, if it rises to an unprecedented level, may negatively affect Macau’s high-end segments (i.e. direct VIP and high-end premium mass); and (3) slower-than-expected ramp-up at the Londoner.

Galaxy Entertainment: Our Dec-24 PT of HK\$43 is based on a property by property sum-of-the-parts valuation that implies a 13.6x FY24E EV/EBITDA, compared to its historical mid-cycle multiple range of 13-15x. The multiple is higher than what we assume for its peers (11-13x), which is justified in our view given its best-in-class business mix and future growth opportunities. Upside risks include: 1) stronger-than-expected ramp-up of Phase 3; and 2) YTD stock underperformance may start to reverse, if there are signs that new product ramp-up would accelerate meaningfully. Downside risks include: 1) deteriorating macro and consumption environments in China could weigh on gaming demand, as well as investment sentiment/valuation; 2) a potential clampdown on capital outflow, which, if it rises to an unprecedented level, may negatively affect Macau‘s highend segments (i.e. direct VIP and high-end premium mass); and 3) slower-than-expected ramp-up in Galaxy Macau Phase III.

• Melco Resorts (covered by Joe Greff in the US): Our year-end 2024 price target remains \$10 and is based on an SOTP approach on 2025E EV/EBITDA, ascribing (1) an 8x target multiple to City of Dreams Macau, (2) 8x to Studio City (at MLCO’s 55% stake), (3) 7x to Mocha, Cyprus and Altira, and (4) 7.0x to its interests in Manila. At current levels, MLCO trades at 7.2x our 2024E EV/EBITDA and 6.4x our 2025E EV/EBITDA. Downside risks: (1) Macau reopening is slower and more restrictive than expected, which would result in a slower-than-anticipated recovery; (2) tighter regulatory policies have a great impact on sentiment, valuation multiples, and multi-year EBITDA trajectory; and (3) capital return is more subdued than expected or required investment in Macau is greater than expected.

SJM Holdings: Our Dec-24 PT of HK\$3.30 is based on a property-by-property sum-of-the-parts valuation that implies an 11.4x FY24E EV/EBITDA, compared to its historical mid-cycle multiple range of 11-12x. We view the multiple as fair compared to peers’ (11-13x). Downside risks include: (1) a deteriorating macro and consumption landscape in China, which could weigh on gaming demand, as well as investment sentiment/valuation; (2) a potential clampdown on capital outflows, which, if it rises to an unprecedented level, may negatively affect Macau’s high end segments (i.e., direct VIP and high-end premium mass); and (3) a slower-than-expected ramp-up of GLP. Upside risks include: (1) a strongerthan-expected ramp-up of GLP; and (2) the severe stock underperformance may start to reverse, especially if there are signs that GLP ramp-up could accelerate meaningfully. 86 J.P.Morgan

NR is Not Rated. In this case, J.P.
